Oh, hello. Thank you so much for fitting me in, doctor. Um... well, I suppose I'm here because I got a little... overly enthusiastic recently. Is it alright if I just explain what happened? 

I volunteer with the historical society, and I guess I sort of took on this big project like I could run the whole fundraiser all by myself. I just really wanted to help them out, you know? But I started making all these new quilt designs, and calling volunteers, and writing up schedules... and I ended up promising deadlines I couldn't possibly meet. 

It sounds a bit foolish saying it out loud, doesn't it? I just wanted to do a good job for everyone, but I suppose I might have overextended myself a little bit.

Oh, goodness, no. I really don't think I felt superior to anyone... I would hate to think I made any of the other ladies feel that way. 

I just... I suppose I just felt very inspired, you know? Not that I have any special talents that no one else has. Everyone at the society is so wonderful and gifted in their own ways. Well... yes. I suppose I have. I hope you don't think I'm just feeling sorry for myself, but... ever since the quilt orders started falling behind and all those awful conflicts with the other volunteers blew up, I've just felt so terribly low. 

It's been quite a while, actually. I think it's been about nine months now that I've been stuck in this... this sort of guilty, sad place. I try my best to put on a brave face, of course, because I don't want to burden anyone else with my feelings. But it's just been so hard to shake off the shame of it all. 

Does that make sense? I know I should just pick myself up and move on, but it's been difficult. I hope you don't think I'm being overly dramatic about it?

Well... yes, I suppose there have been quite a few days like that. I really try not to let it show, because I know how important it is to keep busy and be productive, but...

I felt so perfectly capable, you know? Like I had this special vision for the fundraiser and I didn't need to bother anyone else with the work. \n\nDoes that make sense? I really just wanted to make everyone proud, but I suppose it sounds terribly arrogant now, doesn't it?\n\nDoctor: Did you feel like you were superior to others or had special talents that no one else had during that time?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no. I really don't think I felt superior to anyone... I would hate to think I made any of the other ladies feel that way. \n\nI just... I suppose I just felt very inspired, you know? Not that I have any special talents that no one else has. Everyone at the society is so wonderful and gifted in their own ways. I just happened to have a lot of energy for my specific project at that moment, and I got a little carried away with my own excitement. \n\nI certainly didn't mean to think I was better than anyone else... I hope you know I'm not that kind of person?\n\nDoctor: Have you had other times in your life where you felt this overly enthusiastic and took on more than you could handle?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, no, I don't believe so. I mean, I've always been a bit of an eager beaver, I suppose, when it comes to helping out in the community. But... no, I wouldn't say I've ever gotten quite this tangled up in my own plans before. \n\nUsually, I'm very careful about my commitments. I hate letting people down, you see. I suppose I have. I think it's been about nine months now that I've been stuck in this... this sort of guilty, sad place. I try my best to put on a brave face, of course, because I don't want to burden anyone else with my feelings. But it's just been so hard to shake off the shame of it all. \n\nDoes that make sense? I know I should just pick myself up and move on, but it's been difficult. I hope you don't think I'm being overly dramatic about it?\n\nDoctor: Have you had days where you didn't feel like doing anything because of these low feelings?\n\nPatient: Well... yes, I suppose there have been quite a few days like that. I really try not to let it show, because I know how important it is to keep busy and be productive, but... \n\nIt's just that things I usually enjoy so much... well, they just don't seem to hold the same appeal right now. Well... yes, I suppose my sleep did get a little... unusual during that time. I really try to maintain a proper schedule, you know, early to bed and early to rise. But for a while there... I was only sleeping a few hours a night. 

I'd wake up at two or three in the morning, and I wouldn't feel tired at all! I felt completely ready to start sewing or to make phone calls—though I did try to wait until a decent hour to call anyone, I promise. It just felt like I had all the energy in the world and sleep was just getting in the way. 

But now... oh, it's quite the opposite, and it's so frustrating. I either lie awake in the dark just worrying about everything, or I sleep in terribly late and still wake up feeling completely worn out. It's not like me at all to be so inconsistent. Do you think it's normal for sleep to change so drastically like that? I'm trying very hard to get back to a sensible routine.

But... no, I wouldn't say I've ever gotten quite this tangled up in my own plans before. \n\nUsually, I'm very careful about my commitments. I hate letting people down, you see. This recent business with the historical society was really just an exception... a bit of a silly mistake on my part. I haven't made a habit of it, I promise. Is that... is that the sort of thing you were asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods of feeling unusually down or depressed after this incident with the historical society?\n\nPatient: Well... yes. I suppose I have. I hope you don't think I'm just feeling sorry for myself, but... ever since the quilt orders started falling behind and all those awful conflicts with the other volunteers blew up, I've just felt so terribly low. \n\nIt's been quite a while, actually. I think it's been about nine months now that I've been stuck in this... this sort of guilty, sad place. I try my best to put on a brave face, of course, because I don't want to burden anyone else with my feelings. But it's just been so hard to shake off the shame of it all. \n\nDoes that make sense? I know I should just pick myself up and move on, but it's been difficult. I hope you don't think I'm being overly dramatic about it?\n\nDoctor: Have you had days where you didn't feel like doing anything because of these low feelings?\n\nPatient: Well... yes, I suppose there have been quite a few days like that. I really try not to let it show, because I know how important it is to keep busy and be productive, but... \n\nIt's just that things I usually enjoy so much... well, they just don't seem to hold the same appeal right now. Like my garden, for instance. I used to love going out every morning to check on my plants, and I've always enjoyed working on my own personal quilt entries. But lately... I just look at the fabric scraps or the new seed catalogs that come in the mail, and I just feel... nothing much at all. \n\nI know it sounds terrible, and I really am trying to snap out of it. I don't want to be one of those people who just sits around doing nothing. I hope you understand that I'm trying my best?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt abnormally happy or irritable and had a lot of energy?\n\nPatient: Well... I suppose... oh dear, I am a bit embarrassed to admit this. But right around the time I took on all those projects, I did feel... well, almost glowing at first. It felt like every single idea I had was just wonderful, and I had so much energy to get it all done. \n\nBut... and I really hate to say this because I pride myself on being polite... if anyone questioned me or my plans, I got a bit snappish. I hope that doesn't make me sound like a dreadful person?\n\nDoctor: How long did this glowing, energetic feeling last before you started to feel down?\n\nPatient: Well... it's a bit hard to admit because it feels like I was just being terribly bossy for such a long time, but... I felt so perfectly capable, you know? Like I had this special vision for the fundraiser and I didn't need to bother anyone else with the work. \n\nDoes that make sense? I really just wanted to make everyone proud, but I suppose it sounds terribly arrogant now, doesn't it?\n\nDoctor: Did you feel like you were superior to others or had special talents that no one else had during that time?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no. I really don't think I felt superior to anyone... I would hate to think I made any of the other ladies feel that way. \n\nI just... I suppose I just felt very inspired, you know? Not that I have any special talents that no one else has. Everyone at the society is so wonderful and gifted in their own ways. I just happened to have a lot of energy for my specific project at that moment, and I got a little carried away with my own excitement. \n\nI certainly didn't mean to think I was better than anyone else... I hope you know I'm not that kind of person?\n\nDoctor: Have you had other times in your life where you felt this overly enthusiastic and took on more than you could handle?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, no, I don't believe so. I mean, I've always been a bit of an eager beaver, I suppose, when it comes to helping out in the community. But... no, I wouldn't say I've ever gotten quite this tangled up in my own plans before. \n\nUsually, I'm very careful about my commitments. I hate letting people down, you see. This recent business with the historical society was really just an exception... a bit of a silly mistake on my part. I haven't made a habit of it, I promise. Is that... is that the sort of thing you were asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods of feeling unusually down or depressed after this incident with the historical society?\n\nPatient: Well... yes. I suppose I have. I hope you don't think I'm just feeling sorry for myself, but... ever since the quilt orders started falling behind and all those awful conflicts with the other volunteers blew up, I've just felt so terribly low. \n\nIt's been quite a while, actually. I think it's been about nine months now that I've been stuck in this... this sort of guilty, sad place. I try my best to put on a brave face, of course, because I don't want to burden anyone else with my feelings. But it's just been so hard to shake off the shame of it all. \n\nDoes that make sense? I know I should just pick myself up and move on, but it's been difficult. I hope you don't think I'm being overly dramatic about it?\n\nDoctor: Have you had days where you didn't feel like doing anything because of these low feelings?\n\nPatient: Well... yes, I suppose there have been quite a few days like that. I really try not to let it show, because I know how important it is to keep busy and be productive, but... \n\nIt's just that things I usually enjoy so much... well, they just don't seem to hold the same appeal right now. Like my garden, for instance. I used to love going out every morning to check on my plants, and I've always enjoyed working on my own personal quilt entries. But lately... I just look at the fabric scraps or the new seed catalogs that come in the mail, and I just feel... nothing much at all. \n\nI know it sounds terrible, and I really am trying to snap out of it. I don't want to be one of those people who just sits around doing nothing. I hope you understand that I'm trying my best?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt abnormally happy or irritable and had a lot of energy?\n\nPatient: Well... I suppose... oh dear, I am a bit embarrassed to admit this. But right around the time I took on all those projects, I did feel... well, almost glowing at first. It felt like every single idea I had was just wonderful, and I had so much energy to get it all done. \n\nBut... and I really hate to say this because I pride myself on being polite... if anyone questioned me or my plans, I got a bit snappish. I sort of acted like they were just standing in the way of progress. I feel awful about it now, of course, because I'm usually very even-tempered. I don't know what got into me. I hope that doesn't make me sound like a dreadful person?\n\nDoctor: How long did this glowing, energetic feeling last before you started to feel down?\n\nPatient: Well... it's a bit hard to admit because it feels like I was just being terribly bossy for such a long time, but... I suppose that whole overdriven stretch went on for about five months. \n\nI really thought I was just being wonderfully productive, you know? It wasn't until everything fell apart that I realized how out of hand it had gotten. Five months is quite a long time to be acting like that, isn't it? I do hope you don't think too poorly of me for letting it go on for so long before I realized there was a problem. I really am usually much more considerate of others.\n\nDoctor: Did you have any periods where you didn't need much sleep, but still felt rested and had a lot of energy?\n\nPatient: Well... yes, I suppose my sleep did get a little... unusual during that time. I really try to maintain a proper schedule, you know, early to bed and early to rise. But for a while there... I was only sleeping a few hours a night. \n\nI'd wake up at two or three in the morning, and I wouldn't feel tired at all! I felt completely ready to start sewing or to make phone calls—though I did try to wait until a decent hour to call anyone, I promise. It just felt like I had all the energy in the world and sleep was just getting in the way. \n\nBut now... oh, it's quite the opposite, and it's so frustrating. I either lie awake in the dark just worrying about everything, or I sleep in terribly late and still wake up feeling completely worn out. It's not like me at all to be so inconsistent. Do you think it's normal for sleep to change so drastically like that? I'm trying very hard to get back to a sensible routine.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in your appetite since you started feeling low?\n\nPatient: Well... I suppose my appetite has been fairly normal. I try to make sure I eat properly, you know, well-balanced meals. I don't think there's been any major change... at least, nothing I can really point to. I mean, maybe I haven't been quite as interested in cooking elaborate things lately, but I still make sure I have something sensible. That's the right thing to do, isn't it? I wouldn't want to let my nutrition slip just because I'm feeling a bit down.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Well... I suppose it's a bit of a mix, really. I do try very hard to put on a brave face, especially if I have errands to run or if I'm speaking with a neighbor. I try my best to put on a brave face, of course, because I don't want to burden anyone else with my feelings. But it's just been so hard to shake off the shame of it all. \n\nDoes that make sense? I know I should just pick myself up and move on, but it's been difficult. I hope you don't think I'm being overly dramatic about it?\n\nDoctor: Have you had days where you didn't feel like doing anything because of these low feelings?\n\nPatient: Well... yes, I suppose there have been quite a few days like that. I really try not to let it show, because I know how important it is to keep busy and be productive, but... \n\nIt's just that things I usually enjoy so much... well, they just don't seem to hold the same appeal right now. Like my garden, for instance. I used to love going out every morning to check on my plants, and I've always enjoyed working on my own personal quilt entries. But lately... I just look at the fabric scraps or the new seed catalogs that come in the mail, and I just feel... nothing much at all. \n\nI know it sounds terrible, and I really am trying to snap out of it. I don't want to be one of those people who just sits around doing nothing. I hope you understand that I'm trying my best?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt abnormally happy or irritable and had a lot of energy?\n\nPatient: Well... I suppose... oh dear, I am a bit embarrassed to admit this. But right around the time I took on all those projects, I did feel... well, almost glowing at first. It felt like every single idea I had was just wonderful, and I had so much energy to get it all done. \n\nBut... and I really hate to say this because I pride myself on being polite... if anyone questioned me or my plans, I got a bit snappish. I sort of acted like they were just standing in the way of progress. I feel awful about it now, of course, because I'm usually very even-tempered. I don't know what got into me. I hope that doesn't make me sound like a dreadful person?\n\nDoctor: How long did this glowing, energetic feeling last before you started to feel down?\n\nPatient: Well... it's a bit hard to admit because it feels like I was just being terribly bossy for such a long time, but... I suppose that whole overdriven stretch went on for about five months. \n\nI really thought I was just being wonderfully productive, you know? It wasn't until everything fell apart that I realized how out of hand it had gotten. Five months is quite a long time to be acting like that, isn't it? I do hope you don't think too poorly of me for letting it go on for so long before I realized there was a problem. I really am usually much more considerate of others.\n\nDoctor: Did you have any periods where you didn't need much sleep, but still felt rested and had a lot of energy?\n\nPatient: Well... yes, I suppose my sleep did get a little... unusual during that time. I really try to maintain a proper schedule, you know, early to bed and early to rise. But for a while there... I was only sleeping a few hours a night. \n\nI'd wake up at two or three in the morning, and I wouldn't feel tired at all! I felt completely ready to start sewing or to make phone calls—though I did try to wait until a decent hour to call anyone, I promise. It just felt like I had all the energy in the world and sleep was just getting in the way. \n\nBut now... oh, it's quite the opposite, and it's so frustrating. I either lie awake in the dark just worrying about everything, or I sleep in terribly late and still wake up feeling completely worn out. It's not like me at all to be so inconsistent. Do you think it's normal for sleep to change so drastically like that? I'm trying very hard to get back to a sensible routine.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in your appetite since you started feeling low?\n\nPatient: Well... I suppose my appetite has been fairly normal. I try to make sure I eat properly, you know, well-balanced meals. I don't think there's been any major change... at least, nothing I can really point to. I mean, maybe I haven't been quite as interested in cooking elaborate things lately, but I still make sure I have something sensible. That's the right thing to do, isn't it? I wouldn't want to let my nutrition slip just because I'm feeling a bit down.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in your weight since you started feeling low?\n\nPatient: Well, no... my clothes all still seem to fit just the same. I do try to keep a close eye on the scale, just to be sure I'm taking proper care of myself. I know it can be terribly easy to let yourself go when you're feeling... well, under the weather, but I really wouldn't want to do that. \n\nSo, I believe my weight has stayed quite steady. That's a good sign, isn't it? I do hope it shows that I'm at least keeping my physical health in order, even if my mood is a bit of a struggle right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices or seen things that others couldn't hear or see?\n\nPatient: Oh, my goodness, no. Goodness, no, nothing like that. I mean, I know sometimes people's minds can play tricks on them when they're very stressed, but... no, I haven't experienced anything of that sort. \n\nI try to keep a very firm grip on reality, you know? I wouldn't want you to think I was losing my mind or anything... quite that severe. I'm just feeling very sad and tired right now. It's quite normal not to have anything like that happen, right? I just want to reassure you that I'm perfectly lucid.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your low feelings stay with you all day, or are there times when you feel a bit better?\n\nPatient: Well... I suppose it's a bit of a mix, really. I do try very hard to put on a brave face, especially if I have errands to run or if I'm speaking with a neighbor. I wouldn't want anyone to worry, you see. So, I suppose there are moments where I can pull myself together and feel... well, perhaps a little more like my usual self, just for a short while. \n\nBut if I'm being perfectly honest... when I'm alone, the sadness does seem to settle in quite heavily. It's mostly there in the background throughout the day. I do try to stay positive, though. I make a point to open the curtains and let the light in. That's the right approach, don't you think? I'm really trying my best not to just give in to it.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh... I don't really think so. I haven't noticed one part of the day being particularly worse than another, to be perfectly honest. 

I try very hard to just keep a steady routine, you know? Getting up, making my tea, doing a bit of tidying around the house... I find that keeping busy stops me from dwelling on things too much. So I couldn't really point to a specific hour where it all falls apart, because I don't let it. 

I hope that's alright? I know some people say mornings are dreadful for them, but I just try to take each part of the day as it comes and make the best of it. Does that sound like a healthy way to handle it? I really am trying to do the right things.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well... I suppose it does cross my mind. I try not to dwell, of course, because I know we all make mistakes, but... yes, if I'm being perfectly honest, it does weigh on me quite a bit. 

I just keep going over every late order in my head... and, oh dear, every sharp thing I said to the volunteers. I feel just dreadful about how I might have come across. I keep telling myself that I let the church down, and the historical society... honestly, sometimes it feels like I let this whole town down. 

I know that probably sounds a bit dramatic, and I really am trying to move past it. I just hate the thought of disappointing people, you see. It's normal to feel a little guilty when you haven't been your best self, isn't it? I just want to make sure I'm looking at it the right way.

I really don't think I felt superior to anyone... I would hate to think I made any of the other ladies feel that way. \n\nI just... I suppose I just felt very inspired, you know? But... no, I wouldn't say I've ever gotten quite this tangled up in my own plans before. \n\nUsually, I'm very careful about my commitments. I hate letting people down, you see. This recent business with the historical society was really just an exception... a bit of a silly mistake on my part. I haven't made a habit of it, I promise. Is that... is that the sort of thing you were asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods of feeling unusually down or depressed after this incident with the historical society?\n\nPatient: Well... yes. I suppose I have. I hope you don't think I'm just feeling sorry for myself, but... ever since the quilt orders started falling behind and all those awful conflicts with the other volunteers blew up, I've just felt so terribly low. \n\nIt's been quite a while, actually. I think it's been about nine months now that I've been stuck in this... this sort of guilty, sad place. I try my best to put on a brave face, of course, because I don't want to burden anyone else with my feelings. But it's just been so hard to shake off the shame of it all. \n\nDoes that make sense? I know I should just pick myself up and move on, but it's been difficult. I hope you don't think I'm being overly dramatic about it?\n\nDoctor: Have you had days where you didn't feel like doing anything because of these low feelings?\n\nPatient: Well... yes, I suppose there have been quite a few days like that. I really try not to let it show, because I know how important it is to keep busy and be productive, but... \n\nIt's just that things I usually enjoy so much... well, they just don't seem to hold the same appeal right now. Like my garden, for instance. I used to love going out every morning to check on my plants, and I've always enjoyed working on my own personal quilt entries. But lately... I just look at the fabric scraps or the new seed catalogs that come in the mail, and I just feel... nothing much at all. \n\nI know it sounds terrible, and I really am trying to snap out of it. I don't want to be one of those people who just sits around doing nothing. I hope you understand that I'm trying my best?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt abnormally happy or irritable and had a lot of energy?\n\nPatient: Well... I suppose... oh dear, I am a bit embarrassed to admit this. But right around the time I took on all those projects, I did feel... well, almost glowing at first. It felt like every single idea I had was just wonderful, and I had so much energy to get it all done. \n\nBut... and I really hate to say this because I pride myself on being polite... if anyone questioned me or my plans, I got a bit snappish. I sort of acted like they were just standing in the way of progress. I feel awful about it now, of course, because I'm usually very even-tempered. I don't know what got into me. I hope that doesn't make me sound like a dreadful person?\n\nDoctor: How long did this glowing, energetic feeling last before you started to feel down?\n\nPatient: Well... it's a bit hard to admit because it feels like I was just being terribly bossy for such a long time, but... I suppose that whole overdriven stretch went on for about five months. \n\nI really thought I was just being wonderfully productive, you know? It wasn't until everything fell apart that I realized how out of hand it had gotten. Five months is quite a long time to be acting like that, isn't it? I do hope you don't think too poorly of me for letting it go on for so long before I realized there was a problem. I really am usually much more considerate of others.\n\nDoctor: Did you have any periods where you didn't need much sleep, but still felt rested and had a lot of energy?\n\nPatient: Well... yes, I suppose my sleep did get a little... unusual during that time. I really try to maintain a proper schedule, you know, early to bed and early to rise. But for a while there... I was only sleeping a few hours a night. \n\nI'd wake up at two or three in the morning, and I wouldn't feel tired at all! I felt completely ready to start sewing or to make phone calls—though I did try to wait until a decent hour to call anyone, I promise. It just felt like I had all the energy in the world and sleep was just getting in the way. \n\nBut now... oh, it's quite the opposite, and it's so frustrating. I either lie awake in the dark just worrying about everything, or I sleep in terribly late and still wake up feeling completely worn out. It's not like me at all to be so inconsistent. Do you think it's normal for sleep to change so drastically like that? I mean, maybe I haven't been quite as interested in cooking elaborate things lately, but I still make sure I have something sensible. That's the right thing to do, isn't it? I wouldn't want to let my nutrition slip just because I'm feeling a bit down.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in your weight since you started feeling low?\n\nPatient: Well, no... my clothes all still seem to fit just the same. I do try to keep a close eye on the scale, just to be sure I'm taking proper care of myself. I know it can be terribly easy to let yourself go when you're feeling... well, under the weather, but I really wouldn't want to do that. \n\nSo, I believe my weight has stayed quite steady. That's a good sign, isn't it? I do hope it shows that I'm at least keeping my physical health in order, even if my mood is a bit of a struggle right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices or seen things that others couldn't hear or see?\n\nPatient: Oh, my goodness, no. I'm trying very hard to get back to a sensible routine.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in your appetite since you started feeling low?\n\nPatient: Well... I suppose my appetite has been fairly normal. I try to make sure I eat properly, you know, well-balanced meals. I don't think there's been any major change... at least, nothing I can really point to. I mean, maybe I haven't been quite as interested in cooking elaborate things lately, but I still make sure I have something sensible. That's the right thing to do, isn't it? I wouldn't want to let my nutrition slip just because I'm feeling a bit down.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in your weight since you started feeling low?\n\nPatient: Well, no... my clothes all still seem to fit just the same. I do try to keep a close eye on the scale, just to be sure I'm taking proper care of myself. I know it can be terribly easy to let yourself go when you're feeling... well, under the weather, but I really wouldn't want to do that. \n\nSo, I believe my weight has stayed quite steady. That's a good sign, isn't it? I do hope it shows that I'm at least keeping my physical health in order, even if my mood is a bit of a struggle right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices or seen things that others couldn't hear or see?\n\nPatient: Oh, my goodness, no. Well... I try to, I really do. I know how important it is to keep up with hobbies and things like that. But if I'm being completely honest... it has been a little difficult lately. 

I used to just love checking on my garden every morning, and working on my own quilt entries for the fair. It was always such a joy. But recently... I don't know, I just look at the fabric pieces, or the new seed catalogs when they come in the mail, and I just feel... well, nothing much at all. It's silly, isn't it? I know I should be excited about them. 

I still try to sit down with my sewing basket sometimes, just to go through the motions, because I know it's good for me. I just don't have that same spark for it right now. I'm sure it will come back though, don't you think? It's probably just a phase I need to push through. I really am trying not to let it get the better of me.

But... no, I wouldn't say I've ever gotten quite this tangled up in my own plans before. \n\nUsually, I'm very careful about my commitments. I hate letting people down, you see. This recent business with the historical society was really just an exception... a bit of a silly mistake on my part. I haven't made a habit of it, I promise. I hope you don't think I'm just feeling sorry for myself, but... ever since the quilt orders started falling behind and all those awful conflicts with the other volunteers blew up, I've just felt so terribly low. \n\nIt's been quite a while, actually. I think it's been about nine months now that I've been stuck in this... this sort of guilty, sad place. I try my best to put on a brave face, of course, because I don't want to burden anyone else with my feelings. But it's just been so hard to shake off the shame of it all. \n\nDoes that make sense? I know I should just pick myself up and move on, but it's been difficult. I hope you don't think I'm being overly dramatic about it?\n\nDoctor: Have you had days where you didn't feel like doing anything because of these low feelings?\n\nPatient: Well... yes, I suppose there have been quite a few days like that. I really try not to let it show, because I know how important it is to keep busy and be productive, but... \n\nIt's just that things I usually enjoy so much... well, they just don't seem to hold the same appeal right now. Like my garden, for instance. I used to love going out every morning to check on my plants, and I've always enjoyed working on my own personal quilt entries. But lately... I just look at the fabric scraps or the new seed catalogs that come in the mail, and I just feel... nothing much at all. \n\nI know it sounds terrible, and I really am trying to snap out of it. I don't want to be one of those people who just sits around doing nothing. I hope you understand that I'm trying my best?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt abnormally happy or irritable and had a lot of energy?\n\nPatient: Well... I suppose... oh dear, I am a bit embarrassed to admit this. But right around the time I took on all those projects, I did feel... well, almost glowing at first. It felt like every single idea I had was just wonderful, and I had so much energy to get it all done. \n\nBut... and I really hate to say this because I pride myself on being polite... if anyone questioned me or my plans, I got a bit snappish. I sort of acted like they were just standing in the way of progress. I feel awful about it now, of course, because I'm usually very even-tempered. I don't know what got into me. I hope that doesn't make me sound like a dreadful person?\n\nDoctor: How long did this glowing, energetic feeling last before you started to feel down?\n\nPatient: Well... it's a bit hard to admit because it feels like I was just being terribly bossy for such a long time, but... I suppose that whole overdriven stretch went on for about five months. \n\nI really thought I was just being wonderfully productive, you know? It wasn't until everything fell apart that I realized how out of hand it had gotten. Five months is quite a long time to be acting like that, isn't it? I do hope you don't think too poorly of me for letting it go on for so long before I realized there was a problem. I really am usually much more considerate of others.\n\nDoctor: Did you have any periods where you didn't need much sleep, but still felt rested and had a lot of energy?\n\nPatient: Well... yes, I suppose my sleep did get a little... unusual during that time. I really try to maintain a proper schedule, you know, early to bed and early to rise. But for a while there... I was only sleeping a few hours a night. \n\nI'd wake up at two or three in the morning, and I wouldn't feel tired at all! I felt completely ready to start sewing or to make phone calls—though I did try to wait until a decent hour to call anyone, I promise. It just felt like I had all the energy in the world and sleep was just getting in the way. \n\nBut now... oh, it's quite the opposite, and it's so frustrating. I either lie awake in the dark just worrying about everything, or I sleep in terribly late and still wake up feeling completely worn out. It's not like me at all to be so inconsistent. Do you think it's normal for sleep to change so drastically like that? I'm trying very hard to get back to a sensible routine.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in your appetite since you started feeling low?\n\nPatient: Well... I suppose my appetite has been fairly normal. I try to make sure I eat properly, you know, well-balanced meals. I don't think there's been any major change... at least, nothing I can really point to. I mean, maybe I haven't been quite as interested in cooking elaborate things lately, but I still make sure I have something sensible. That's the right thing to do, isn't it? I wouldn't want to let my nutrition slip just because I'm feeling a bit down.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in your weight since you started feeling low?\n\nPatient: Well, no... my clothes all still seem to fit just the same. I do try to keep a close eye on the scale, just to be sure I'm taking proper care of myself. I know it can be terribly easy to let yourself go when you're feeling... well, under the weather, but I really wouldn't want to do that. \n\nSo, I believe my weight has stayed quite steady. That's a good sign, isn't it? I do hope it shows that I'm at least keeping my physical health in order, even if my mood is a bit of a struggle right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices or seen things that others couldn't hear or see?\n\nPatient: Oh, my goodness, no. Goodness, no, nothing like that. I mean, I know sometimes people's minds can play tricks on them when they're very stressed, but... no, I haven't experienced anything of that sort. \n\nI try to keep a very firm grip on reality, you know? I wouldn't want you to think I was losing my mind or anything... quite that severe. I'm just feeling very sad and tired right now. It's quite normal not to have anything like that happen, right? I just want to reassure you that I'm perfectly lucid.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your low feelings stay with you all day, or are there times when you feel a bit better?\n\nPatient: Well... I suppose it's a bit of a mix, really. I do try very hard to put on a brave face, especially if I have errands to run or if I'm speaking with a neighbor. I wouldn't want anyone to worry, you see. I'm really trying my best not to just give in to it.\n\nDoctor: Are there any specific times of the day when your low feelings seem to be worse than others?\n\nPatient: Oh... I don't really think so. I haven't noticed one part of the day being particularly worse than another, to be perfectly honest. \n\nI try very hard to just keep a steady routine, you know? Getting up, making my tea, doing a bit of tidying around the house... I find that keeping busy stops me from dwelling on things too much. So I couldn't really point to a specific hour where it all falls apart, because I don't let it. \n\nI hope that's alright? I really am trying to do the right things.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself thinking about what you could have done differently with the historical society project?\n\nPatient: Well... I suppose it does cross my mind. I feel just dreadful about how I might have come across. I keep telling myself that I let the church down, and the historical society... honestly, sometimes it feels like I let this whole town down. \n\nI know that probably sounds a bit dramatic, and I really am trying to move past it. I just hate the thought of disappointing people, you see. It's normal to feel a little guilty when you haven't been your best self, isn't it? I just want to make sure I'm looking at it the right way.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any days where you don't feel low at all?\n\nPatient: Well... I hope you don't think I'm usually this boastful, but... at the time, I truly believed my heritage quilt ideas were going to put our little town on the map. \n\nI just had this wonderful feeling like I had finally found the thing that only I could make happen. I felt so perfectly capable, you know? Like I had this special vision for the fundraiser and I didn't need to bother anyone else with the work. \n\nDoes that make sense? I really just wanted to make everyone proud, but I suppose it sounds terribly arrogant now, doesn't it?\n\nDoctor: Did you feel like you were superior to others or had special talents that no one else had during that time?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no. I really don't think I felt superior to anyone... I would hate to think I made any of the other ladies feel that way. \n\nI just... I suppose I just felt very inspired, you know? Not that I have any special talents that no one else has. Everyone at the society is so wonderful and gifted in their own ways. I just happened to have a lot of energy for my specific project at that moment, and I got a little carried away with my own excitement. \n\nI certainly didn't mean to think I was better than anyone else... I hope you know I'm not that kind of person?\n\nDoctor: Have you had other times in your life where you felt this overly enthusiastic and took on more than you could handle?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, no, I don't believe so. I mean, I've always been a bit of an eager beaver, I suppose, when it comes to helping out in the community. But... no, I wouldn't say I've ever gotten quite this tangled up in my own plans before. \n\nUsually, I'm very careful about my commitments. I hate letting people down, you see. This recent business with the historical society was really just an exception... a bit of a silly mistake on my part. I haven't made a habit of it, I promise. Is that... is that the sort of thing you were asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods of feeling unusually down or depressed after this incident with the historical society?\n\nPatient: Well... yes. I suppose I have. I hope you don't think I'm just feeling sorry for myself, but... ever since the quilt orders started falling behind and all those awful conflicts with the other volunteers blew up, I've just felt so terribly low. \n\nIt's been quite a while, actually. I think it's been about nine months now that I've been stuck in this... this sort of guilty, sad place. I try my best to put on a brave face, of course, because I don't want to burden anyone else with my feelings. But it's just been so hard to shake off the shame of it all. \n\nDoes that make sense? I know I should just pick myself up and move on, but it's been difficult. I hope you don't think I'm being overly dramatic about it?\n\nDoctor: Have you had days where you didn't feel like doing anything because of these low feelings?\n\nPatient: Well... yes, I suppose there have been quite a few days like that. I really try not to let it show, because I know how important it is to keep busy and be productive, but... \n\nIt's just that things I usually enjoy so much... well, they just don't seem to hold the same appeal right now. Like my garden, for instance. I used to love going out every morning to check on my plants, and I've always enjoyed working on my own personal quilt entries. But lately... I just look at the fabric scraps or the new seed catalogs that come in the mail, and I just feel... nothing much at all. \n\nI know it sounds terrible, and I really am trying to snap out of it. I don't want to be one of those people who just sits around doing nothing. I hope you understand that I'm trying my best?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt abnormally happy or irritable and had a lot of energy?\n\nPatient: Well... I suppose... oh dear, I am a bit embarrassed to admit this. But right around the time I took on all those projects, I did feel... well, almost glowing at first. It felt like every single idea I had was just wonderful, and I had so much energy to get it all done. \n\nBut... and I really hate to say this because I pride myself on being polite... if anyone questioned me or my plans, I got a bit snappish. I sort of acted like they were just standing in the way of progress. I feel awful about it now, of course, because I'm usually very even-tempered. I don't know what got into me. I hope that doesn't make me sound like a dreadful person?\n\nDoctor: How long did this glowing, energetic feeling last before you started to feel down?\n\nPatient: Well... it's a bit hard to admit because it feels like I was just being terribly bossy for such a long time, but... I suppose that whole overdriven stretch went on for about five months. \n\nI really thought I was just being wonderfully productive, you know? It wasn't until everything fell apart that I realized how out of hand it had gotten. Five months is quite a long time to be acting like that, isn't it? I do hope you don't think too poorly of me for letting it go on for so long before I realized there was a problem. I really am usually much more considerate of others.\n\nDoctor: Did you have any periods where you didn't need much sleep, but still felt rested and had a lot of energy?\n\nPatient: Well... yes, I suppose my sleep did get a little... unusual during that time. I really try to maintain a proper schedule, you know, early to bed and early to rise. But for a while there... I was only sleeping a few hours a night. \n\nI'd wake up at two or three in the morning, and I wouldn't feel tired at all! I felt completely ready to start sewing or to make phone calls—though I did try to wait until a decent hour to call anyone, I promise. It just felt like I had all the energy in the world and sleep was just getting in the way. \n\nBut now... oh, it's quite the opposite, and it's so frustrating. I either lie awake in the dark just worrying about everything, or I sleep in terribly late and still wake up feeling completely worn out. It's not like me at all to be so inconsistent. I wouldn't want to let my nutrition slip just because I'm feeling a bit down.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in your weight since you started feeling low?\n\nPatient: Well, no... my clothes all still seem to fit just the same. That's a good sign, isn't it? I mean, I know sometimes people's minds can play tricks on them when they're very stressed, but... no, I haven't experienced anything of that sort. \n\nI try to keep a very firm grip on reality, you know? I wouldn't want you to think I was losing my mind or anything... quite that severe. I'm just feeling very sad and tired right now. It's quite normal not to have anything like that happen, right? I just want to reassure you that I'm perfectly lucid.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your low feelings stay with you all day, or are there times when you feel a bit better?\n\nPatient: Well... I suppose it's a bit of a mix, really. I do try very hard to put on a brave face, especially if I have errands to run or if I'm speaking with a neighbor. I wouldn't want anyone to worry, you see. So I couldn't really point to a specific hour where it all falls apart, because I don't let it. \n\nI hope that's alright? I know some people say mornings are dreadful for them, but I just try to take each part of the day as it comes and make the best of it. Does that sound like a healthy way to handle it? I really am trying to do the right things.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself thinking about what you could have done differently with the historical society project?\n\nPatient: Well... I just wanted to do a good job for everyone, but I suppose I might have overextended myself a little bit.\n\nDoctor: Did you feel like you were capable of handling all those tasks by yourself at the time you took them on?\n\nPatient: Well... yes, I really did. It’s a little embarrassing to admit, actually. I hope you don't think I'm usually this boastful, but... at the time, I truly believed my heritage quilt ideas were going to put our little town on the map. \n\nI just had this wonderful feeling like I had finally found the thing that only I could make happen. I felt so perfectly capable, you know? Like I had this special vision for the fundraiser and I didn't need to bother anyone else with the work. \n\nDoes that make sense? I really just wanted to make everyone proud, but I suppose it sounds terribly arrogant now, doesn't it?\n\nDoctor: Did you feel like you were superior to others or had special talents that no one else had during that time?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no. I really don't think I felt superior to anyone... I would hate to think I made any of the other ladies feel that way. \n\nI just... I suppose I just felt very inspired, you know? Not that I have any special talents that no one else has. Everyone at the society is so wonderful and gifted in their own ways. I just happened to have a lot of energy for my specific project at that moment, and I got a little carried away with my own excitement. \n\nI certainly didn't mean to think I was better than anyone else... I hope you know I'm not that kind of person?\n\nDoctor: Have you had other times in your life where you felt this overly enthusiastic and took on more than you could handle?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, no, I don't believe so. I mean, I've always been a bit of an eager beaver, I suppose, when it comes to helping out in the community. But... no, I wouldn't say I've ever gotten quite this tangled up in my own plans before. \n\nUsually, I'm very careful about my commitments. I hate letting people down, you see. This recent business with the historical society was really just an exception... a bit of a silly mistake on my part. I haven't made a habit of it, I promise. Is that... is that the sort of thing you were asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods of feeling unusually down or depressed after this incident with the historical society?\n\nPatient: Well... yes. I suppose I have. I hope you don't think I'm just feeling sorry for myself, but... ever since the quilt orders started falling behind and all those awful conflicts with the other volunteers blew up, I've just felt so terribly low. \n\nIt's been quite a while, actually. I think it's been about nine months now that I've been stuck in this... this sort of guilty, sad place. I try my best to put on a brave face, of course, because I don't want to burden anyone else with my feelings. But it's just been so hard to shake off the shame of it all. \n\nDoes that make sense? I know I should just pick myself up and move on, but it's been difficult. I hope you don't think I'm being overly dramatic about it?\n\nDoctor: Have you had days where you didn't feel like doing anything because of these low feelings?\n\nPatient: Well... yes, I suppose there have been quite a few days like that. I really try not to let it show, because I know how important it is to keep busy and be productive, but... \n\nIt's just that things I usually enjoy so much... well, they just don't seem to hold the same appeal right now. Like my garden, for instance. I used to love going out every morning to check on my plants, and I've always enjoyed working on my own personal quilt entries. But lately... I just look at the fabric scraps or the new seed catalogs that come in the mail, and I just feel... nothing much at all. \n\nI know it sounds terrible, and I really am trying to snap out of it. I don't want to be one of those people who just sits around doing nothing. I hope you understand that I'm trying my best?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt abnormally happy or irritable and had a lot of energy?\n\nPatient: Well... I suppose... oh dear, I am a bit embarrassed to admit this. But right around the time I took on all those projects, I did feel... well, almost glowing at first. It felt like every single idea I had was just wonderful, and I had so much energy to get it all done. \n\nBut... and I really hate to say this because I pride myself on being polite... if anyone questioned me or my plans, I got a bit snappish. I sort of acted like they were just standing in the way of progress. I feel awful about it now, of course, because I'm usually very even-tempered. I don't know what got into me. I hope that doesn't make me sound like a dreadful person?\n\nDoctor: How long did this glowing, energetic feeling last before you started to feel down?\n\nPatient: Well... it's a bit hard to admit because it feels like I was just being terribly bossy for such a long time, but... I suppose that whole overdriven stretch went on for about five months. \n\nI really thought I was just being wonderfully productive, you know? It wasn't until everything fell apart that I realized how out of hand it had gotten. Five months is quite a long time to be acting like that, isn't it? I do hope you don't think too poorly of me for letting it go on for so long before I realized there was a problem. I really am usually much more considerate of others.\n\nDoctor: Did you have any periods where you didn't need much sleep, but still felt rested and had a lot of energy?\n\nPatient: Well... yes, I suppose my sleep did get a little... unusual during that time. I really try to maintain a proper schedule, you know, early to bed and early to rise. But for a while there... I was only sleeping a few hours a night. \n\nI'd wake up at two or three in the morning, and I wouldn't feel tired at all! I felt completely ready to start sewing or to make phone calls—though I did try to wait until a decent hour to call anyone, I promise. It just felt like I had all the energy in the world and sleep was just getting in the way. \n\nBut now... oh, it's quite the opposite, and it's so frustrating. I either lie awake in the dark just worrying about everything, or I sleep in terribly late and still wake up feeling completely worn out. It's not like me at all to be so inconsistent. Do you think it's normal for sleep to change so drastically like that? I'm trying very hard to get back to a sensible routine.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in your appetite since you started feeling low?\n\nPatient: Well... I suppose my appetite has been fairly normal. I try to make sure I eat properly, you know, well-balanced meals. I don't think there's been any major change... at least, nothing I can really point to. I mean, maybe I haven't been quite as interested in cooking elaborate things lately, but I still make sure I have something sensible. That's the right thing to do, isn't it? I wouldn't want to let my nutrition slip just because I'm feeling a bit down.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in your weight since you started feeling low?\n\nPatient: Well, no... my clothes all still seem to fit just the same. I do try to keep a close eye on the scale, just to be sure I'm taking proper care of myself. I know it can be terribly easy to let yourself go when you're feeling... well, under the weather, but I really wouldn't want to do that. \n\nSo, I believe my weight has stayed quite steady. That's a good sign, isn't it? I do hope it shows that I'm at least keeping my physical health in order, even if my mood is a bit of a struggle right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices or seen things that others couldn't hear or see?\n\nPatient: Oh, my goodness, no. Goodness, no, nothing like that. I mean, I know sometimes people's minds can play tricks on them when they're very stressed, but... no, I haven't experienced anything of that sort. \n\nI try to keep a very firm grip on reality, you know? I wouldn't want you to think I was losing my mind or anything... quite that severe. I'm just feeling very sad and tired right now. It's quite normal not to have anything like that happen, right? I just want to reassure you that I'm perfectly lucid.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your low feelings stay with you all day, or are there times when you feel a bit better?\n\nPatient: Well... I suppose it's a bit of a mix, really. I do try very hard to put on a brave face, especially if I have errands to run or if I'm speaking with a neighbor. I wouldn't want anyone to worry, you see. So, I suppose there are moments where I can pull myself together and feel... well, perhaps a little more like my usual self, just for a short while. \n\nBut if I'm being perfectly honest... when I'm alone, the sadness does seem to settle in quite heavily. It's mostly there in the background throughout the day. I do try to stay positive, though. I make a point to open the curtains and let the light in. That's the right approach, don't you think? I'm really trying my best not to just give in to it.\n\nDoctor: Are there any specific times of the day when your low feelings seem to be worse than others?\n\nPatient: Oh... I don't really think so. I haven't noticed one part of the day being particularly worse than another, to be perfectly honest. \n\nI try very hard to just keep a steady routine, you know? Getting up, making my tea, doing a bit of tidying around the house... I find that keeping busy stops me from dwelling on things too much. So I couldn't really point to a specific hour where it all falls apart, because I don't let it. \n\nI hope that's alright? I know some people say mornings are dreadful for them, but I just try to take each part of the day as it comes and make the best of it. Does that sound like a healthy way to handle it? I really am trying to do the right things.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself thinking about what you could have done differently with the historical society project?\n\nPatient: Well... I suppose it does cross my mind. I try not to dwell, of course, because I know we all make mistakes, but... yes, if I'm being perfectly honest, it does weigh on me quite a bit. \n\nI just keep going over every late order in my head... and, oh dear, every sharp thing I said to the volunteers. I feel just dreadful about how I might have come across. I keep telling myself that I let the church down, and the historical society... honestly, sometimes it feels like I let this whole town down. \n\nI know that probably sounds a bit dramatic, and I really am trying to move past it. I just hate the thought of disappointing people, you see. It's normal to feel a little guilty when you haven't been your best self, isn't it? I just want to make sure I'm looking at it the right way.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any days where you don't feel low at all?\n\nPatient: Well... I suppose there aren't many days where I feel completely, one hundred percent cheerful right now. I mean, I don't think anyone is happy every single day, are they? \n\nI do have moments, like I said, where I feel quite capable and fine... especially when I'm keeping busy and doing what needs to be done. But a whole day without any of that heaviness at all? I'm not entirely sure. I try my very best to make every day a good one, I really do. I hope you don't think I'm just sitting around feeling sorry for myself, because I'm absolutely not. I'm trying to be strong about it all. That's what you're supposed to do, right?\n\nDoctor: Are you able to enjoy or find interest in activities you normally like to do, even on your worst days?\n\nPatient: Well... I try to, I really do. I know how important it is to keep up with hobbies and things like that. But if I'm being completely honest... it has been a little difficult lately. \n\nI used to just love checking on my garden every morning, and working on my own quilt entries for the fair. It was always such a joy. But recently... I don't know, I just look at the fabric pieces, or the new seed catalogs when they come in the mail, and I just feel... well, nothing much at all. It's silly, isn't it? I know I should be excited about them. \n\nI still try to sit down with my sewing basket sometimes, just to go through the motions, because I know it's good for me. I just don't have that same spark for it right now. I'm sure it will come back though, don't you think? It's probably just a phase I need to push through. I really am trying not to let it get the better of me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have moments during the day where you feel completely free of these low feelings?\n\nPatient: Well... I think we might have just talked about this a little bit, didn't we? I hope I didn't confuse you before. \n\nBut, um... no, I suppose I don't really have moments where it completely lifts. Not right now, anyway. It's just sort of... always there in the background, even when I'm trying my hardest to focus on something else. \n\nI don't want to sound like a broken record, complaining all the time. I know I need to just keep putting one foot in front of the other. It's perfectly normal to have a rough patch where things just feel heavy all day long, isn't it? I'm sure I'll bounce back soon. I really am doing my best to keep a positive attitude about it all.\n\nDoctor: Have your low feelings lasted for most of the day, nearly every day, for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness... two weeks? I suppose I haven't really been keeping track on a calendar or anything like that. Like I mentioned, it does feel like it's been lingering in the background lately, but... I don't want you to think I've just been completely incapacitated for weeks on end, because I'm truly doing my best to keep the house tidy and stay on top of my chores. \n\nHas it been most of the day, nearly every day for that long? I'm honestly not entirely sure. It's so hard to measure these things when you're just trying to push through and be productive, isn't it? I hope it's alright that I don't have an exact timeline for you... I just try not to dwell on the negative too much, so I haven't really been counting the days. Am I making sense?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

The patient's reported feelings of low mood, loss of interest in activities, and changes in sleep patterns are more consistent with depressive symptoms, making F32.0 a stronger candidate. F34.0 remains a possibility due to the patient's description of persistent feelings of guilt and worthlessness, which could be indicative of a persistent depressive disorder.", "is_final": false}

I know I should just pick myself up and move on, but it's been difficult. I hope you don't think I'm being overly dramatic about it?\n\nDoctor: Have you had days where you didn't feel like doing anything because of these low feelings?\n\nPatient: Well... yes, I suppose there have been quite a few days like that. I really try not to let it show, because I know how important it is to keep busy and be productive, but... \n\nIt's just that things I usually enjoy so much... well, they just don't seem to hold the same appeal right now. Like my garden, for instance. I used to love going out every morning to check on my plants, and I've always enjoyed working on my own personal quilt entries. But lately... I just look at the fabric scraps or the new seed catalogs that come in the mail, and I just feel... nothing much at all. \n\nI know it sounds terrible, and I really am trying to snap out of it. I don't want to be one of those people who just sits around doing nothing. I hope you understand that I'm trying my best?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt abnormally happy or irritable and had a lot of energy?\n\nPatient: Well... It felt like every single idea I had was just wonderful, and I had so much energy to get it all done. \n\nBut... and I really hate to say this because I pride myself on being polite... if anyone questioned me or my plans, I got a bit snappish. I sort of acted like they were just standing in the way of progress. I feel awful about it now, of course, because I'm usually very even-tempered. I don't know what got into me. I hope that doesn't make me sound like a dreadful person?\n\nDoctor: How long did this glowing, energetic feeling last before you started to feel down?\n\nPatient: Well... it's a bit hard to admit because it feels like I was just being terribly bossy for such a long time, but... I suppose that whole overdriven stretch went on for about five months. \n\nI really thought I was just being wonderfully productive, you know? It wasn't until everything fell apart that I realized how out of hand it had gotten. Five months is quite a long time to be acting like that, isn't it? I do hope you don't think too poorly of me for letting it go on for so long before I realized there was a problem. I really am usually much more considerate of others.\n\nDoctor: Did you have any periods where you didn't need much sleep, but still felt rested and had a lot of energy?\n\nPatient: Well... yes, I suppose my sleep did get a little... unusual during that time. I really try to maintain a proper schedule, you know, early to bed and early to rise. But for a while there... I was only sleeping a few hours a night. \n\nI'd wake up at two or three in the morning, and I wouldn't feel tired at all! I felt completely ready to start sewing or to make phone calls—though I did try to wait until a decent hour to call anyone, I promise. It just felt like I had all the energy in the world and sleep was just getting in the way. \n\nBut now... oh, it's quite the opposite, and it's so frustrating. I either lie awake in the dark just worrying about everything, or I sleep in terribly late and still wake up feeling completely worn out. It's not like me at all to be so inconsistent. That's a good sign, isn't it? Goodness, no, nothing like that. I'm really trying my best not to just give in to it.\n\nDoctor: Are there any specific times of the day when your low feelings seem to be worse than others?\n\nPatient: Oh... I don't really think so. Getting up, making my tea, doing a bit of tidying around the house... I find that keeping busy stops me from dwelling on things too much. So I couldn't really point to a specific hour where it all falls apart, because I don't let it. \n\nI hope that's alright? I know some people say mornings are dreadful for them, but I just try to take each part of the day as it comes and make the best of it. Does that sound like a healthy way to handle it? I really am trying to do the right things.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself thinking about what you could have done differently with the historical society project?\n\nPatient: Well...
